ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e5111	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Dear ladies and gentlemen! We want to start this National Council Day with a welcome topic, namely the analysis of the European Council, chaired by Federal Chancellor Schüssel. This day we will still have enough to deal with the scandals around the BAWAG and a red network in Austria. But, ladies and gentlemen, this is a less encouraging chapter. Therefore, let us not first look at the Caribbean, but at Brussels! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We've got on 23/24 in the last week. In March, a European Council chaired by Austria, which, I believe, has also made very decisive points. I therefore do not understand – and may say so at the outset – that small opposition politicians today say that nothing has been specifically decided there. Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, leave it! If Austria has the Presidency in Europe and really brings good results home, then leave these small settings! It has really been decided great things, and Austria has been able to profile itself in Europe. And we should all be happy about this! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We have already given a clear signal to the question that a Presidency has to determine, namely which issues will be discussed at such a summit: Employment, growth and energy. Employment and growth – these are the pressing issues that every Austrian and European citizen raises today because it is important to him that his children, that his grandchildren, and that he himself have a job in a work process and can be successful there. This is why employment and growth are the key issue that Austria has put at the top of the list of priorities. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Take a look at the concrete decisions on this. These can actually be seen. With regard to employment, under the Austrian Presidency, the body of the highest Heads of State and Government in Europe has decided that each year we want to create two million jobs, which, broken down to every Member State, means that there is another perspective there, that there is employment and that there are programmes for achieving such employment, ladies and gentlemen. [[Abg. Oils: Which one?]] That's not supposed to be a concrete goal? – This is a very concrete goal that gives many people hope again. I should like to explain how we have done this in Austria. We have already presented an employment programme. Last year we decided a package for more employment of € 285 million, in addition to for 2006. What's going on? – It is not only money spent; it is precisely tried to create work where work is requested by promoting training in the health professions by promoting training in the care professions by providing re-entry assistance. These are very concrete measures, which will now result in 25 employment programmes in a large European context, thus effectively combating unemployment at its root. That's what we want! And that is why this is a great success for Austria. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Or: Look at the specific goals for young people who may not have a job at all! By 2007 – as decided by the European Council – the issue of young school leavers being given a job will be given priority by setting themselves the goal of providing a job to a young school leaver within six months, within a year, to provide an apprenticeship, to provide a training opportunity. This means that young people get a very concrete perspective. That's not supposed to be a concrete goal? – Ladies and gentlemen! This is an ambitious plan, which must first be implemented everywhere, but within a year, until 2007, that must stand. I think that this is a decisive step forward for Europe as a whole and therefore I very much welcome it. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] What do we have to show Austrians in Europe? What have we done? – Ladies and gentlemen! In Austria, we have specifically launched an apprenticeship offensive with the so-called Blum-Bonus. Each company that creates additional apprenticeships receives a special bonus from the state. What did that do? Today, we can all be pleased that 9 000 additional teaching places have already been created in Austria, i.e. young people have a concrete perspective. And that is particularly important to us. At European level, in the European context, this means that there will be such an offensive in 25 Member States. In 25 Member States, therefore, young people who do not have a job today have a concrete job prospects in the future, because national programmes must ensure that this perspective stands by the end of 2007. And that's what we want: Jobs for young people in Europe! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Let's look at the topic of growth! If we in Europe today want to have an opportunity to prove ourselves on the world market, to succeed in this difficult competition, then we need concrete measures. In which sector? – In the research and development sector. We have a high level of education. We can play this card. Therefore, we must make every effort to make more money available in this sector. Therefore, there is the very concrete decision of the European Council that by 2010 expenditure on research and development will have to rise to 3 percent. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, these are very concrete goals – and not any talk about the future. Each Member State must present an ambitious programme in order for it to be able to provide research and development, and for that purpose funds will be made available. And this also means that by 2010 we will spend €100 billion more on research than today. These are very specific figures – and this will also make a decisive contribution to the competitiveness of Austria. This is a concrete goal – and that is what we want in Europe: more growth. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Let's look at the energy sector: At the beginning of the Austrian EU Presidency, we had to see painfully how much we depend on energy supplies from non-European countries. At the beginning of the year, at the beginning of the Austrian EU Presidency, there was an energy crisis in Europe, as 50 percent of gas supplies from Russia no longer flowed. But with all of us, as I mean, this has led to a phase of uproar – rather painful. And that is why the Austrian EU Presidency – rightly – took the issue of energy as a priority at the last spring summit. In Europe, we need to get away from this dependence, we need ambitious targets to promote renewable energy sources and to play a greater role in the energy process than before. – And: said, done! Look The results can be found at: Quite specific objectives have been formulated in this context, and an action plan on energy efficiency has also been drawn up, in which we will continue to save through technology promotion, especially where energy may be consumed to a too high degree today. Furthermore, there is the very concrete plan that renewable energy should have reached a brand of 15 percent by 2015. A very ambitious and perfectly right goal, as I mean. We in Austria have already done a lot about this: Look For example, you are only looking at our biomass projects; as is the production of energy with hydropower in our country! However, this also needs a European perspective. In any case, these concrete objectives show that sustainability is for the first time a major issue in Europe – and that is what we want to achieve in the energy sector: not to be dependent, but to promote renewable energy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Overall, ladies and gentlemen, this means that German Chancellor Dr. Schüssel has chosen the right topics with his EU Presidency – and that he has shown a Presidency that can really be seen. This must be brought together first that these concrete objectives will be implemented through decisions of the European Council. And of course these measures also bear an Austrian manuscript. Look, ladies and gentlemen, what we have done in terms of employment and the fight against youth unemployment, how we are trying to get hold of this! Look at what we have done in the direction of growth, research and development! Austria is in third place in the European Union today! It's a brand that someone needs to imitate us. Therefore: We have, as I think, every reason to be satisfied in this respect too. On the side of the opposition in Austria, it is criticized that not all that is a problem in Europe has been done. – I can only say, ladies and gentlemen: After three months of our presidency, no one can expect it either! Let me judge someone in the Austrian Presidency who is not considered to be a great friend of Austria. Jacques Chirac, President of France, said that the Austrian Presidency was excellent and very well prepared in terms of ideas, dynamics and leadership. According to Chirac, the summit had taken place under the best conditions and the concrete decisions were proof that Europe was moving forward. Ladies and gentlemen! Federal Chancellor Dr. Schüssel is not only a good Austrian head of government, but now also a statesman of European formats.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e5248	Mr President. High National Council! I should like to comment briefly on the European Council. At the beginning, many issues and objectives that we have set ourselves were very controversial. When we met in the main committee last week, it was true that practically all concrete joint projects were rejected by a majority of the Member States or have not yet been accepted. However, our team has succeeded in emphasizing for all our constant representatives to the EU now, Ambassador Gregor Woschnagg, the Foreign Minister, all the ministers of expertise, that is, the whole team, all the objectives we have set ourselves in the conclusions of the European Council. And that is something that has not been taken for granted! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] A second one: We have made it quite clear that we want to raise the Austrian model of good cooperation – not always without discussions, but genuine, honest cooperation – between the various levels of government and social partners to the European level, so to speak. That is why, for the first time, we have drawn the representatives of the European social partners to the deliberations of the European Council. The representatives of European trade unions, Méndez, and the representatives of European employers, Seillière, together with the President of the European Central Bank – also a premiere – presented their ideas on how we can stimulate growth and employment in Europe. I believe that there is already the Austrian manuscript to be seen, something that hopefully makes school, because together we are undoubtedly stronger than when everyone acts on their own. So what has been agreed in these deliberations, ladies and gentlemen, first of all in the area of growth and employment, a subject which has also been discussed at this spring summit? – We are not satisfied with the presentation of the first six years of the Lisbon Strategy. But now we have a historic opportunity: a growing economy, and optimism in the large EU countries is growing. There may be a special situation in France, but otherwise, as I think, there is quite good expectations. We have done our utmost to re-establish two million additional jobs each year by means of appropriate measures, especially with regard to the framework conditions of the soon 27 EU Member States, which means employment growth of about 1 percent per year. This would result in 10 million jobs by the year 2010. A very important and concrete goal – and this is also achievable, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Of course, this is not to be created by the governments or by the Commission – as I am not changing my line at all – because jobs are created by businesses, especially by medium-sized enterprises, a subject which was discussed widely for the first time at European level. There are 23 million small and medium-sized enterprises in the EU – and these are our hopes in terms of employment and additional growth policies, especially in terms of additional jobs. If we make it easier for these companies to work, then hope is alive. Again: It's an attainable goal! Take only Austria, one of the countries that is always referred to as a model, as well as Denmark, Finland or Sweden. Austria is one of these three, four EU Member States. In Austria we are expected to have an employment growth of 1.3 percent this year. So: 1 per cent employment growth for all can be achieved, with measures that we have long implemented in Austria. And many of the issues that we have dealt with are now in the European work programme. For example, 85 percent of all 22-year-olds are expected to have completed general higher education. By the end of 2007, every young European should be offered either a job, a apprenticeship or a training opportunity – even within four months by the end of 2010. Other objective: Reduce the number of school dropouts to 10 percent. In my opinion, this is one of the most important points that we do not forget young people in this growth and employment strategy, but rather – on the contrary – allow them all the opportunities of the world! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In the field of research, therefore, very concrete support, namely through the European Institute of Technology, although at first very controversial. There has actually been exactly the same discussion as in Austria: The European universities have feared that money will be taken away from them, the Max Planck Institute, for example, or other institutions, have expressed concern that parallel bureaucracies would arise, and so on. – We have reached a yes to the EIT, the European Institute of Technology, but: There must be no mega-bureaucracy, but it has to be a slim and powerful network. And we will be right today with the decision on the Austrian IST, the Institute of Science and Technology, because with it we have succeeded in following this European strategy, something that we have always proposed, as you know. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The common objective is also very concrete: 3 percent for research. That was far, far away – we are now 2%. For the first time we succeeded – in turn a thank you, Mr. Vice-Chancellor, and the Minister of Education – that we have recorded in an Annex what each country has to do in order to achieve this common goal – an increase in research growth per year. In addition, we have also undertaken to move the middle class particularly to the centre. We want to implement lower fees, simplified reporting requirements in statistics, an important and at the same time annoyed issue for all small and medium-sized enterprises. We have given the Commission a mandate to present predictable and quantifiable directives on how to reduce the administrative costs for small businesses. This has been excellent for the Dutch, and we will consider whether we want to transfer this model, which is now also being examined by the Commission, to Austria. We want to allow a young businessman to set up a business within a week; we want to create a central point of contact, a one-stop shop by 2007. The Commission has even agreed to a longer period of resistance, the level of the so-called de minimis rule, below which no The Commission will review the internal market with a view to doubling it. Thus, a long-term demand of Austria is fulfilled for the first time. We have taken the European Investment Bank into account. It will now spend significantly more money on research, trans-European networks and SMEs, for SMEs and R & D €15 billion in addition, and for energy efficiency and trans-European networks €25 billion in addition. Quite specific measures that will help achieve the goal of additional 2 million jobs per year. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Of course, the debate on the new energy policy was very exciting. This is a new topic, certainly also caused by the “resurrection call” at the beginning of the year when the gas supplies of Russia were halved. Federal Minister Martin Bartenstein has been able to restore the supply capacity to full within four days through very clever and prudent negotiations. It was a very good interaction with the Commission, with Russia, with Ukraine, with all areas. To debate this issue of the new energy policy, to strengthen security of supply, to increase competitiveness and to improve environmental quality, to improve and to improve environmental efficiency, was of course a very exciting discussion, a discussion initiated by Angela Merkel. We have set ourselves some specific objectives: First: Cross-border electricity trade is expected to reach 10 percent, which is an important issue because of the need to strengthen the networks, of course, because we can help each other better in a crisis case too. There have already been a number of blackouts, in Switzerland, in Hesse, in Italy, in Great Britain. So this is a very important area. Next goal: Strengthening and increasing the total share of renewable energy to 15 percent – very controversial initially. We are at the moment far away from this goal, but it can be reached if we, as it were, clear the brake gap and allow more power plants. In the field of hydropower in particular, huge reserves are broken. By the way, we can free up the possibilities of private capital to the €1 000 billion for the expansion of the networks, for new power plants and for pipelines. There are job opportunities inside, which can now be made easier with these decisions in the back. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We have undertaken to significantly increase the use of biofuels to 8 percent. To be honest, I would have been even more ambitious, but I admit that there is still a lot of research to be done. The second generation of bio-fuels is to be expected. We need new engines. The current ones are broken if you mix more than 5 or almost 6 percent. But there is still potential in this area. Furthermore, a very important decision: Together we want to make an energy saving of about 20 percent, so that we can make the most of it. Ladies and gentlemen! For the next time – and this is still part of this Council – we have undertaken, especially now in the trilogue, to create more clarity through the new financial perspective for the years 2007 to 2013. This is a very important issue. The Minister of Finance will hold crucial talks with all the countries, Parliament and the Commission during these days. We are preparing a subsidiarity conference to be held the week after Easter in St. Pölten, just under the Austrian Presidency. The enlargement debate, in particular the question of the European Union's capacity to accept, which the Foreign Minister has always called for, is now being accepted by everyone and is to be secured at a special summit of Foreign Ministers on the future of Europe at the end of April/beginning of May. We want not only to discuss the European model of life but also to write it down in concrete terms. And we want the 9th. In May, as a European day, such a broad discussion would be useful. We are working on the establishment of a European Fundamental Rights Agency in Vienna. Perhaps even the Institute for Equal Treatment – the “gender equality pact” – could be established there. And then, in summer, we want to present a choreography for the next steps in the process of the European Constitution. Ladies and gentlemen, we have a rich work programme! And we're not bad on the road, as I mean. Many things have already happened, such as this week's extremely interesting conclusion of European driving licence, European Road Costs Directive, issues that have not been completed since many, many presidencies, but: We still have a lot of work in front of us! We count on your support!

We, in my capacity as judge, have experienced it all the time: On Friday the women were beaten and went to court, paid the man ad. I think that's very, very important. Women may not go to court so quickly or not so often, but once they go to court, they must also expect that the man who beat them will also be held responsible. The term “stalking ” comes from the hunter’s language and means as much as“ panging at a prey ”; we say “wild people. ” Stalkers are indeed wild because they suffer from personality disorders and have a low sense of self - worth. Although Stalker is more likely to be classified as ill, the persecuted must be protected by law. Australian scientists divide the stalkers into five categories. On the one hand, it is ex-partner – that is the largest group – and on the other hand close relatives or friends who want to force reconciliation through a targeted persecution. There are also those stalkers who are in love with their victims and who, through persistent persecution, try to establish an intimate relationship. This group usually has mental disorders. There are stalkers trying to get a meeting with people they're not in love with. This group tends to self-esteem and is narratively concerned. For these stalkers it is not understandable why their victims meet with rejection. Anxious stalkers spread fear and horror through harassment. Often professional groups such as lawyers, doctors or teachers are persecuted by them because they believe they have harmed them. These stalkers belong to the category of paranoids. The last category includes persecutors who have set themselves the goal of attacking their victims on a daily basis. People affected are usually persecuted for weeks and months. Rape occurs frequently in this group. These stalkers always try to keep control, and tend to be too violent. Unfortunately, the impact of stalking on those concerned is considerable. We have already heard a lot about it; the example of Mrs Riener was really impressive. Two thirds of the victims are plagued by sleep disorders and nightmares. Nearly 10 percent report fear during the persecution down to panic-like conditions. This law finally ensures that stalking victims are not left alone and that they can also receive professional assistance through proven victim protection facilities. It is therefore incomprehensible and incomprehensible and a pity that the Greens do not go here.

January 2003, Austria undertakes to make a change in the National Criminal Code and in the Criminal Code. Articles 2 and 3 of the Framework Decision make it necessary to amend existing legislation or add new paragraphs. It must be said that these amendments only make it necessary to fulfil the obligations of international law. The requirements on the government template can be measured by the priorities of environmental crime in the Member States. These are, for example: Any damage or at least potential risks posed by environmental media, certain harmful to humans or the environment, or at least dangerous behaviours of illegal handling of hazardous waste or radioactive substances, and the operation of hazardous installations and illegal behaviour in connection with protected species animals and plants and with the ozone layer, if committed intentionally or grossly negligently. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The existing intent crimes will be adapted in terms of their scope of protection. For example, I would like to mention the extension in the area of the protection of monuments and other things. On the other hand, as already mentioned, negligence offences must also be inserted. New criminal provisions are created here; Criminal provisions against the negligent illicit use of nuclear material or radioactive substances as well as the negligent environmentally harmful operation of installations. It remains elements of the Criminal Code as dangerous offences or potential hazards, but are adapted and inserted in respect of the legal goods to be protected. Thank you.

By redesigning the patient’s supply, clear and transparent arrangements are to be established to enable patients to refuse medical treatment even if they lose their ability to act. We too have long called for the possibility of establishing a binding patient facility so that, if the patient is still fully aware of his perceptiveness and judgment, he can determine which treatment he wishes to refuse. Patients gain influence by this possibility on their future medical treatment, and their will is still observed if they are no longer able to express it themselves. The first essential defect we see is the formal obstacles or strict formal regulations. (b) the establishment of the same before a lawyer, notary or a legal employee of the patient's representatives. In addition, after five years the patient supply must be renewed. For example, the patient's attorney's office in Tyrol is only responsible for the hospitals, so it can't work for established doctors or for patients who have their diseases treated there or who want to establish their order there. Furthermore, the patient's offices are very different, both in terms of personnel and in terms of their field of activity. Another weakness of this government template is that the costs of establishing a patient supply are relatively high, on the basis of the fact that it is necessary to establish it before a notary or before a lawyer. They are estimated at 100 to 300 €. – My colleague Puswald will express himself in detail. In our view, another major weakness of this government template is that, in addition to the high cost of going to the notary or to the lawyer for many citizens in our state, it is not a matter of course, but it still means an obstacle or a great threshold for them, so that it will rather be neglected. As a last point, I would like to address the question of an appropriate register; a point that seems very important to me. It must be mentioned that this government template does not provide for registration of binding patient provision. ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e9787	Madam President. Mrs Gastinger! High house! First of all, a few words about the history of this law: In response to the legalisation of the death aid in Holland, Austria has also made a clear confession – a four-party confession – against the active death aid, but for the death accompaniment and in this episode also carried out a parliamentary survey in May 2001 in order to demonstrate solidarity with the dying and to discuss aspects of a human death accompaniment in Austria. As a result of this survey, a four-party application was made in December 2001, in which the Federal Ministry of Social Affairs and Health was commissioned to develop a draft law. At that time, Federal Minister Haupt immediately set up a group of experts, which has taken place from 2001 to 2003. Following the amendment of the Ministry's Act, the Ministry of Health has adopted this group of experts, completed a draft law and sent it into review. Further, we have discussed and debated very intensively in particular with the Ministry of Justice, with the Minister of Justice and its experts, but also with the representatives of the Hospiz movement, the organisations that have been working on building up the death support in Austria for years now, and with the Ethics Commission, we have tried to further develop this draft law on the joint Council of Ministers of the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of Health. What was that about? – Patients were also possible so far. However, they were only remarkable, not binding. It is especially thanks to the Caritas Socialis and Caritas, who have developed the instrument, written information brochures, but also made drafts for patient activities. On the whole, however, all of this has moved in the right-hand room to this day and has naturally led to uncertainty among patients, as well as doctors. In this legal regulation, it was important for us to make a clear distinction, in the field of tension between patient autonomy, that is self-determination, and the prohibition of active death aid and, on the other hand, the obligation to make medical decisions, the obligation to treat and also the need for medical treatment, because the patient autonomy limits the obligation to take medical care. – It cannot, however, require active death aid. The debate in this field of tension has been very intense in the last one and a half years. We really didn't make it easy. We have intensively especially with experts who actually with dying and heavy have worked and worked with doctors, but also with lawyers to find the best possible solution. The previous speeches have already shown that each one of us was very affected, also with regard to the question of how abuse can be prevented, how to ensure that dying people are not so to speak driven into the binding patient supply by pressing heirs. Therefore, there was the need for intensive medical advice so that the patient could actually decide, and the need for formal conditions for the binding provision of patients and also for the time-limitation of the binding patient's provision, which is nevertheless at any time revocable by the patient or by the patient himself. – That is, as long as the patient or the patient is able to articulate, he or she may, of course, withdraw a binding patient supply. The written binding provision of patients is particularly valid if the patient or the patient is no longer able to articulate when he or she has lost consciousness. If binding patient provision has already expired in this phase, it is still a considerable patient supply – a highly significant one, for we know that there is a wide range of liability to be decided by the doctor or by the doctor in his or her responsibility. I believe that this law has enabled us to achieve the goal – namely the creation of a clear regulation for patients and also for doctors – and that we have created a wide range of instruments with the distinction between binding and significant. A considerable patient supply does not need this form condition, therefore is not associated with such costs, and we know that in the meantime the Caritas has already completed some 130 000 patient facilities with patients who, of course, continue to be considered significant, also in the sense of high attention. What's important, perhaps, is: Of course, handling in emergency situations is also regulated. The law does not affect medical emergency care provided that the time spent searching for a patient would seriously endanger the life or health of the patient or the patient. Of course, we should also consider, for example, a note on the health card – on the e-card – or subsequently on the electronic life-accompanied health certificate, so that the doctor or doctor has this information available very quickly. I would like to thank you very much, especially Mrs Gastinger and her house for the good cooperation in this law. I would particularly like to thank Mrs Maria Theresia Fekter, who has accompanied us for a long time, including in the development of the draft Council of Ministers, and for Mrs Partik-Pablé, who has also accompanied us, but also for all the members of the opposition for the serious discussion in the Committee, of course also among the experts who have accompanied us on this path. I think that we can now adopt a good law, which will certainly allow for possible improvements in two or three years' evaluation.

And I also believe that this should not be a cost question. ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e11551	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! My previous speakers have it has already been mentioned several times: One of the reasons why we are voting on this Takeover Act today – there are three reasons – is that we are on one side until 20. In May, the Takeover Directive must be transposed into national law, which is very important because non-implementation in this very specific case could also be associated with very high financial consequences for the Republic of Austria. I would like to point this out once again. As you know, as you know, all those who are familiar with the legal matter, the Constitutional Court took a decision in December of the previous year, according to which it will deal with the Takeover Act, and has already announced that there may be concerns about the certainity of certain terms in the Takeover Act. That was also the reason why we wanted to introduce this bill to the Constitutional Court before its final decision, which we are expected to have to take in June of this year. But the third, very important point – and this one has already been mentioned several times by my previous speakers – is certain that in Austria we wanted to ensure legal certainty in the case of larger stock transactions. Why? – Of course, we also want to protect the Austrian core shareholders. We have a special structure in Austria at our listed companies, according to which we have a lot of companies in which Austrian core shareholders are represented with a percentage of 25 percent. It has also been important with this law to protect these Austrian core shareholders and, above all, to ensure legal certainty here in the future. We have therefore separated ourselves from the current legal situation, which is particularly linked to a very high individual case-law for the economy itself, which would be expressed euphemistically. Why “Individual Justice ”? Because, if you have now invested in Austria with us, you did not know, for example, if you have acquired 11 or 12 or 15 percent of a company's share capital, whether the Takeover Commission will then not tell you that you have actually acquired practically a majority of the shares and thus a control over the company, even though you never wanted to. Of course, the situation was the same, especially in passive control acquisition, where you could do less, for example, if you had a stock package of 15 to 16 percent and were, by selling another major shareholder at once, the one who then held a majority, and then at one time were forced to make a takeover offer to all the others from one day to the other, without providing for it financially. So you see that the current legal situation was really linked to a lot of uncertainties. However, the Federal Government is due to legal certainty here, legal certainty also for the economic location Austria, in order to enable investment in the economic location Austria. As I said, The 26 percent threshold, where we now assume that a control acquisition is possible here in Austria, results from the Austrian core shareholders with 25 percent plus one percent. We have also foreseen that if someone now has more than 26 percent share capital, which is to rest beyond voting rights, unless one gets to the committee on takeover requests for certain conditions so that one can also use these voting rights. And a similar arrangement In particular, we have also provided for passive control acquisitions, so that we also ensure legal certainty here. I believe that this law, as it now stands, will certainly contribute a great deal to improving Austria's economic location. And above all, I also believe that, as an economic location in Austria, we will certainly have a very good place in European competition, especially with regard to the stock market and the stock market. Of course, it was also important to us – for all of us are of course also the small shareholders – that we have now removed the 50 percent discount for small shareholders in the takeover offer. This is important because, as Mr Ikrath has said, we have now done – or was it Mrs Partik-Pablé?; they both argued in this direction, which I think is very correct – to the small shareholders show that they are as valuable to us as the big shareholders. As far as the main negotiating presence is concerned, of course, it is also up to the representatives of the small shareholders to ensure greater HV representations here. It cannot, however, be the responsibility of the legislator or the State to intervene in the economy in a manner which regulates the economy. It is important to me that we create framework conditions for the economy, but within these conditions the economy must be able to develop freely. In this sense I hope for your approval and thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e12010	This means we can reach the 8th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker was Mr Kößl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e12038	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen of the House! With today's bill, we are adopting an important treaty between the Benelux countries and Germany, France, Spain and Austria. This Treaty is an important development of cooperation under the Schengen Implementing Convention with a view to improving the fight against cross-border crime, terrorism and illegal migration. So there are serious forms of crime and no bagatellan occasions. International crime can only be combated internationally, can only be successfully combated by close, internationally oriented police work. International crime has set up a network worldwide, and a close network between police organisations must be set up to meet this challenge in the area of crime. It is therefore intended to enable and implement enhanced more intensive cross-border police cooperation. The priorities of the new powers of cooperation are as follows: Storage and exchange of DNA files, storage and exchange of roof tylyscopic data, collection of data from vehicle registers, transmission of information to prevent terrorist offences, aviation security officers, fight against illegal migration, posting of document consultants to countries classified as source or transit states, mutual support in return, assistance in major events, disasters and serious accidents. It is important that all accesses are first made anonymous and only when a hit is made the requesting national contact point is informed. The Convention contains extensive provisions on data protection due to the sensitivity of the data. All data protection concerns could be resolved in advance by this law, and thus we can rightly claim that this Treaty creates conditions for greater security in Europe, for greater security in Austria and for people in our country. Mrs. Minister, thank you and your team for the commitment that this contract has come about.

It is therefore more than sensible to put together and unify resources of these individual cooperation countries. The cooperation model provides for as high a standard as possible in cooperation, particularly in the field of information exchange. In future, DNA profiles, vehicle data, fingerprints and personal data can be transmitted to the Member States and compared with each other. The desired information can be found in minutes on the computer of the investigative – and not as before after a few weeks, and then by post. In the future, each state can access the stored data directly to the persecution of crime. This, ladies and gentlemen, will have a huge positive impact on the investigation rate of crime. It would be desirable, however, that this cooperation between seven countries is the beginning of cooperation between all Schengen countries. We very much expect this Prüm Treaty to combat international crime, terrorism and illegal migration. It must be clear to all of us that there is an ever-increasing risk from these areas internationally. As a single state, however, we can only do something with restrictions, but the data from seven countries can give us a decisive advantage in the investigation work. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Austria is one of the safest countries in the world, and Federal Minister Prokop and Federal Chancellor Wolfgang Schüssel ensure that this will remain the same in the future. Thank you.

Since 2000, the Austrian Federal Government has been fully committed to improving and attracting Austria's economic and labour location. We have set up a number of measures to upgrade this economic and work location – think of the first and second stages of tax reform, redundancies of workers, the economy, research and development, education, infrastructure. Everything with the aim: How can we improve the framework conditions for more jobs, more prosperity and more income for our people? We have been very much committed to upgrading Austria's financial centre, for an attractive capital market. The Austrian Stock Exchange is the best-performing stock exchange of all Western industrialized countries in recent years. There has been a fivefold increase in capitalization, a reduction in sales on the stock market. The index level has quadrupled. We have invested massively in the trustworthiness, the credibility of the site. I should like to remind you of joint decisions here in the House, a capital market offensive law, a capital market representative, an employee care, one of the major social reforms, a provision for the future. We have jointly launched the Financial Market Supervision with Constitutional Law – a decision of all four political groups here in the House – on my proposal and after intensive negotiations, because we were all convinced that there would be a more powerful financial market surveillance. We have passed an economic hygiene law. Finally, we have also decided on an Enforcement package so that financial market supervision has more bit to actually examine and act here. Ladies and gentlemen, remember the last months of reporting and remember that we had media reports: “Austria – the better Germany”. – Think of material reports: The Centre for European Reform has said that Austria is the country with the third highest competitiveness throughout Europe after Sweden and Finland. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen, let me come to answer the questions raised. Question 1: According to the provisions of the Stocks Act, the Board of Directors shall lead the company in such a way as to require the well-being of the company, taking into account the interests of shareholders and employees as well as public interest. The Executive Board shall inform the Supervisory Board regularly, promptly and comprehensively about all relevant business development issues including the risk situation in the company and all Group companies. The task of the Supervisory Board is to control the Executive Board. On the basis of the facts currently known, there is a suspicion that, in addition to the Banking Act, a number of other standards could have been infringed. I may provide the following information: It will be possible to examine possible errors of the Executive Board under the Stocks Act or Commercial Code. For example, it should be noted that: Information obligations to the Supervisory Board, lack of information about the auditor, incomplete information in the annual financial statements or progress report. With regard to possible errors to be suspected under the Criminal Code, for example the obligation of the Executive Board to provide information according to § 255 of the Stock Exchange Act, according to media reports already determined the public prosecutor's office in Vienna. Another point: Possible errors of the Executive Board under the Banking Act will be to be examined. This may involve infringements of the rules relating to large-scale investments, in particular that there could have been no approval by the Supervisory Board, or failure to comply with the annual reporting obligation, insufficient formation of groups of related companies, in particular with regard to the Caribbean companies. It will be necessary to examine a breach of the care requirement in accordance with § 39 of the Banking Act. It will be necessary to examine the incomplete or the deliberately incorrect provision of information vis-à-vis financial market supervision. It will be necessary to examine the lack of qualitative and quantitative features of the internal revision as well as a possible breach of accounting and equity rules in the depreciation of losses arising from the Caribbean transactions. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: Why not 2001?]] There may have been any infringements of the rules on taxation, which will also have to be examined. There may have been errors of the auditor to examine, in particular with regard to insufficient auditing by the auditor. This requires full information on the part of the Board of Directors. It will be necessary to consider whether the auditor was aware of the situation of the Caribbean daughters, the corresponding claims and the guarantee of the Austrian Trade Union Confederation, because a reporting obligation would have existed under § 63 of the Banking Act, which obviously has not been observed. Finally, it will be necessary to examine errors under the association law, because, as we all know from media reports, it is likely that a guarantee from the ÖGB has been given and, of course, the question arises whether this guarantee has been obtained in accordance with the provisions of the association law under the Statutes of the ÖGB and has subsequently also been included in the annual accounts of the Austrian Trade Union Confederation. With regard to infringements of the Banking Act, it is noted that with the recent amendment to the Banking Act, we have significantly increased the administrative penalties and created further effective sanctions mechanisms for infringements. Should it be in breach of § 255 para. 1 of the Stocks Act, then, under certain conditions, prison sentences up to a year or fines up to 360 daily sentences are provided as punishment from the court. For infidelity within the meaning of § 153 of the Criminal Code, a criminal framework of up to 10 years is provided for in the case of damages exceeding € 50 000. In this context, I would like to point out the recent media reports confirmed by the spokesman of the Vienna Public Prosecutor's Office, according to which the Vienna Public Prosecutor's Office has already filed applications for arrest against Phillip Bennett and Wolfgang Flöttl in connection with the BAWAG-REFCO. On Monday, the prosecutor's office had initiated a judicial investigation against the former REFCO chief Bennett and the son of the former long-time BAWAG general director Walter Flöttl for fraud or infidelity. ==References== If the persons responsible have learned from the first Caribbean business in 1994, I am allowed to carry out the following: I believe that if we follow what has now been reported, the responsible have clearly not learned anything from the first Caribbean scandal in 1994. There is a level-building of supervision in Austria. Stage 1 – the Board of Directors: This is, of course, obliged to take care. Stage 2 – an internal revision. Stage 3 – the Supervisory Board of the Bank: Of course, this one has to carry out the bank's control. Stage 4 – the auditor: The main task of the auditor is to assess the value of claims or liabilities, because that is the prerequisite for testing a balance sheet or not. [[Abg. I would, however, like to make a brief comment on what is to be understood here as a matter of fact: Income and thus income within the meaning of § 15 Income Tax Act 1988 are available when the taxable person benefits from its non-business income. Money benefits must be taken into account with the usual average price of the consumer. The most well-known items are, for example, service cars or service apartments. A regulation has been adopted on the subject, including rules on the assessment of housing at square meters per month. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to conclude by saying once again: Our greatest interest must be to secure the bank's future, to give the savers security and, on the other hand, to ensure a complete awareness of these events! - Thank you many times.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e18872	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! So that we can get back to the subject – the debate on the ratification of the Prüm Treaty was interrupted at 3 p.m. – I say again briefly what it is about in the Prüm Treaty. The Prümer contract regulates the exchange of DNA data, roof tyloscopic data, for example fingerprints, and data from the vehicle register. The purpose of this Treaty is to be concluded, if it is finally ratified, between seven EU Member States. With the Prümer contract, it will now be possible to balance open DNA traces. According to expectations – the Minister expects this – in the future, after ratification of the Treaty, about 2,000 crimes are to be more resolved. - We'll see. I would like to say to Mr Pilz's allegations, which were raised in the afternoon against all those who support this Treaty or vote in the vote with Yes: The Prümer Treaty is the first police cooperation agreement in Europe, which provides for concrete data protection provisions associated with appropriate controls. This is good, and this is exemplary in terms of data protection and fundamental rights. The SPÖ will therefore agree to the Treaty in its final version, because the data protection concerns could be resolved and because there is no general access to Austrian data stocks. Data surveys are anonymized and refer to the individual case. In addition, each data query is logged so that the Data Protection Commission can verify the use and transmission of data in each individual case. The opinion of the Data Protection Council was therefore received. The Treaty is therefore without prejudice to data protection law, and it can be agreed in this form. What is a problem, however, and I would like to point out this again – I would also point out this in every discussion we have discussed about police cooperation: Mrs Minister, we are now the leader of the EU Presidency, and I would like to point out that we really are also in the other cooperation areas. In the European Parliament or where it is most useful, agreements between the police should be established. I think that's what I'm going to do. - That's one. Another lack which I would like to highlight is – and this has already been called for several times in the debate today – that, in addition to the seven states that are now trying to ratify, the other remaining states should still ratify this Treaty – not only in order to decide on this legal work, but also in order to avoid different legal situations in Europe, in the EU, that there are different areas of the subject, where a different core European structure is formed. I do not think that is good, we should try to avoid this, because it is important that the EU's fragile political framework exists in a uniform law and that there are equal legal situations, otherwise we are even more endangering the Europe project. At the end of the day, Mrs. Minister, the following: If, with this Treaty, we have created the control bodies – which is good and to be supported – through the Data Protection Commission, it will nevertheless be necessary to provide for the appropriate human resources, because without the appropriate human resources, everything will be useless. In this sense, I hope that you will act on the Federal Chancellor so that compliance with what is law can also be controlled here. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e19076	Thank you, Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Austria has already made significant advances in terms of Europe-wide police cooperation on the defence of terrorism. In May 2005, Germany, the Benelux States, agreed France, Spain and Austria on the so-called Prümer contract – named after the Eifel city in which the contract was signed. On the basis of this contract, the Contracting Parties undertake to exchange information and thus to significantly improve the exchange of information for the purpose of preventing and persecuting crimes between Signatory States. The Treaty provides that the States concerned shall grant each other certain access rights to DNA and fingerprint files as well as to vehicle registers, with particular reference to the fact that the older DNA data that have been processed so far can also be networked internationally. There are 12 000 open traces in Austria, whose clarification is still outstanding. The data balance is now expected to allow hundreds of cases to be re-rolled. Many of these unexplained cases of the past are now faced with the great opportunity to be finally completed, and there is the opportunity to give the victims justice and certainty. Ladies and gentlemen! This Prümer contract is a mosaic in the overall system to increase security in one of the safest countries in Europe. A good law! I'm asking for your consent.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e19731	Mr President. High house! The question was asked by Mr Grünewald: Where does the Austrian Federal Government set priorities? – Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to point this out. Our priorities are the whole field of education. We take a broad education course: from kindergarten to adult education, from compulsory school, from folk school to excellence promotion. We invest in all areas. We help that in kindergartens the children, who cannot yet learn German, learn better German. For this purpose, we have created a new opportunity, with € 640 000, the kindergartens will be supported in their efforts. We invest in elementary schools, in secondary schools. In the last ten years, we have opened two new school buildings each month in the Federal School: Constructions, renovations, expansions. We have invested heavily in universities, in new buildings. We have secured the budget for the universities for the next few years, with an increase of 11 percent, with €500 million for a general renovation. We have doubled the budget of the technical universities, the number of students has doubled. Mr Grünewald, I know you as a fair person. Because you always claim that the number of students has fallen since 1999: You know that there have been a lot of people who have not studied, that we have had realistic figures since 2000 and that these figures are constantly rising, that we are already with over 220 000 students, and that in particular the number of first-level recipients is increasing to a special extent, but that the number of graduates is also increasing to a particular extent. So something has happened, it has developed a little further at our universities. Because you're still asking: Where do we set priorities?, I tell you very clearly: in the field of research. If you look at it, then you will be able to find out: Ten new institutes of the Academy of Sciences were established. The budget of the Academy of Sciences was increased by 80 percent. I emphasize: 80 percent since 1999! That can be seen. The scientific research fund's budget has been increased from €80 million to €150 million this year. And if you know internationally, you know that such science funds support about 33 percent of the projects you are applying for, in order to promote the excellence projects. We have had 31 or 32 percent so far, and through the increase, the promotion will surely be 38 percent. This is a very good support approach. Because you are trying to get the Wittgenstein Prizewinners and also the foreign researchers who have supposedly spoken against the new Excellence Institute in Klosterneuburg, I can tell you: You can also read very carefully otherwise! – You too, Mr Broukal, will otherwise read very carefully. The text of this letter was signed by the speaker, Dr. Schachermayer. And below it is said: Other Wittgenstein prize winners are... And then we called some and found out that they didn't sign. And that is why Wittgenstein Prizewinner Professor Gornik is now also present at the National Committee. He said: I am not against this Excellence Institute at all, I am pleased when this Excellence Institute comes in addition! They also know quite well that we have about 800 young researchers in the United States who work there and work there at universities. A little over 30 have signed a general letter. They were asked to sign this letter and, of course, to make it public. It's always public writing. But I would just like to say to this Excellence Institute: We have had a very fruitful discussion, and that has been my concern from the very beginning. The discussion was started by Professor Zeilinger, who from the beginning spoke of an elite university. A group of researchers, the so-called core team, then developed these thoughts further, and there have been very many discussions. The task of this core team was to present us with an objective assessment of the sites. We have committed international institutes to this, and I have followed this outcome. The task of the core team was to present us with a draft law. You then complained that we did not give this law a long review. But we have de facto had a public discussion, we have de facto had a review – and we have made it better together. I would like to thank all those who have put forward ideas and proposals. It was the idea of the core team to say: We want to have the representatives of the donors and the scientists in different bodies! And it was then the idea to say: It is better as it is at the Weizmann Institute that scientists and the representatives of the lenders are in a curatorium! We have then changed this in this direction, we have introduced additional rules that the Chairperson of the Board of Trustees, until the First President is appointed, manages the business; that the President proposes to the Scientific Council that the Board appoints the Scientific Council; and that after the first appointment of the scientists, the scientists be appointed from the board of trustees. That is, politics, the government no longer has any influence. Ladies and gentlemen! I mean, we're taking a very important step with this Institute of Science and Technology. We take an important step towards further positioning ourselves in the field of excellence. We have areas of excellence, we build areas of excellence. When we think of MedAustron, which is realized in Wiener Neustadt: an area of excellence in the field of medical care, in the field of treatment, in the field of material research, in the field of spallation. We have the Excellence Institutes of the Academy of Sciences, we have Excellence Institutes at our good universities, and we have balancedly made sure that universities get more money, the Science Fund gets more money, and that money is there for this Excellence Institute we want to build. If you look closely at it: The Weizmann Institute has also started small. And if you don't start, then finally no excellence institute can be created in this way. In any case, I would like to thank all those who are now supporting us in the establishment of this Excellence Institute.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e20086	Mr President. An actual correction to the statements made by Mrs Sburny, who claimed here that the Chimpanzee project in Gänserndorf would not be due to a lack of money. She also said that because women are not involved, this is a problem. The fact is that the project with the chimpanzees in Gänserndorf is a project where laboratory monkeys infected with HIV, i.e. AIDS and hepatitis, are still present. can spend their lifetime there, but which are far away from a natural behavior in relation to their group behaviour, through long life in the cage. For this reason, science has also called this project, which was intended as a group behavior study project, inappropriate for these monkeys. So this has nothing to do with the money, but with the monkeys. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e20448	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen! I believe that a knowledge-based society in which we are and which is developing rapidly must be given great attention, especially in training. It is therefore rightly at the heart of the political debate. I also believe that it is necessary to complement the fact that it is important that these highly trained people find employment – if possible in Austria. If I look at the rate of unemployed academics and their increase, I have to say that this is an average of 16 per cent per year since 2000, and this is also too high for a total increase of 5.9 per cent. This development must also be discussed in the context of higher education. However, I would also like to agree with Dr. Grünewald, who asked how to get, so to speak, those people who are suitable for a Center of Excellence. Of course, you need best universities, so you can't create a Center of Excellence or an Institute of Science and Technology without the background of good common universities. I am very glad that this strange discussion about Elite University is finally over, because one has realized that it is actually a postgraduate training facility for particularly interested people in a subject. These institutions can therefore only exist in total networking and in close connection with the parent universities. I have already said this in committee: For me, it would be important to build up such a wreath of centers of excellence around the universities, with appropriate spin-off effects, because only then does the whole thing make sense, where the location is not so important. As a Lower Austrian, I add that I welcome it and it is already okay when the location in Lower Austria is located. It is important that training and research are closely linked to achieve success. It also makes no sense to say now that it is all right for Parliamentary Broukal to spend €30 million on the FWF, because it is equally important to improve the facilities of universities where states are really not easy for the students and do not easily lead to a center of excellence, since some are happy when they have finished their studies. That is why it is important to be aware that one has a whole thing here. I am still thinking of several institutes. I say Seibersdorf now, which leads to a certain shadow existence. Why isn't this a center of excellence for material technology? Why are there not others, such as the Arsenal, who have excellent research results? I'm talking about a wreath of Centers of Excellence located around the main universities, in Innsbruck, Graz and at other locations. This means that you can, as it were, drive a continuous educational rail, up to the highest international peaks. Everyone knows that a tip only arises if you have a certain width of training. That is why I think this separation is preferable, while the other one is a bit dependent on it, is a wrong concept. And I say this very clearly: The concept is only based on creating teaching and learning conditions at the universities, which open up the opportunity to advance to the top education places of Europe and the world. We have to thank God, people in Austria who have this qualification. It is therefore good to build up institutions around these personalities so that they can finally exist in a European context. If I sometimes read about networking between universities and others, I have to say that only those who have never performed anything at a university can say that. Each university lives on the fact that it is highly networked; without networking it would soon become a dry branch. Therefore, the focus is not on the location issue, but on the people and their involvement. I remember the discussion about the Danube University of Krems. That's where Vice Chancellor Busek didn't want to agree at the time. Nevertheless, she shows – she has developed very well – that she has made a right decision. I am convinced that if we give the support – and I am right in giving Members the Green Forest – if we are to ensure appropriate equipment with equipment and resources, then we can also expect great success, since it is closely correlated with these factors. In this sense, I would like to thank you very much for agreeing, at least between the Social Democrats and the Government parties, because I believe it is a good contribution on the way to more knowledge and higher education, yes, to the highest level of education. I wish the Institute of Science and Technology much success.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e20531	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! In a debate contribution was just to be heard, Austria is to become a good location for science and research. I can calm everyone, it is already an excellent location for science and research. This does not require the statement of the State Secretary for Research and Technology Development, but it is international evaluation criteria that lead us to this excellent position, such as the European Innovation Scoreboard, the Innovation Scoreboard: For the year 2005, we are in fifth place within the EU, and in 2003 it was still the tenth place. For example, this Innovation Scoreboard will evaluate – these are criteria! –: Public sector R & D expenditure, business R & D expenditure, graduates of scientific and technical studies, access to broadband, i.e. a range of indicators leading to evaluation. After all, fifth place, trend rising. Lisbon Scorecard VI, commissioned by the EU Commission, assesses us in third place regarding innovation – also increasing trend. Here, for example, indicators of social reforms, prosperity and productivity of a state are important. All the indices show and argue that Austria is an excellent, excellent location for research and development. Finally, of course, the research rate is also another proof of this: We are here on the path of the Lisbon Strategy, on the path 2.5 percent research rate of GDP in 2006, i.e. this year, and 3 percent in 2010. I particularly regret that the Green Group is going into isolation with its opinion; I also argue and justify why this is the case. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) There are extensive transport packages on the agenda. ==References====External links== KFG-Novelle is among other things the winter tire requirement for trucks. In winter, there have been problems with trucks that have stopped on motorways. With this novelle a winter tire requirement is now prescribed by law. ==References====External links== ==References== For trucks and semi-trailers with a total weight of more than 3 500 kilograms. The tyres marked with ‘M+S’ with corresponding profile depth are considered as winter tyres. From my point of view, it is also particularly important in this regard to include snow chains for at least two drive bikes, which we now regulate by law, so that trucks are well equipped for winter driving conditions. One of my main points is that this regulation also applies to foreign vehicles. Let's remember: In November, when there was severe snowfall in Carinthia, Slovenia’s trucks remained suspended. This winter tyre requirement now also applies to foreign trucks. [[Abg. Neudeck: I am for helmet duty on the government bank!]] It has been debated over and over again, and it is interesting: In Slovenia there is also a winter tire requirement, but the Slovenians have regulated this very peculiarly, there is a winter tire requirement there, but you can also drive with summer tires and snow chains. – This “good” Slovenian solution may have been the reason for the trucks to remain with us. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I would like us to talk about a winter tire requirement for cars now. There are always very clear indications that there is also a need for a winter tyre requirement for cars. 95 percent of the drivers drive winter tyres anyway. Personally, I would like you to look at this issue of winter tire duty for cars more closely in the ministry and examine the matter in more detail. In this sense, we will agree to this law.

There are also full-year tires, and there are M+S tyres that do not grasp from a certain degree of hardness, even if the profile depth is given. We are also concerned with road safety, which is no question at all. However, if you make legal provisions, you should design the law in such a way that everyone can stick to it and not then create a discussion as to whether it may have been winter tyres that were no longer suitable or whose profile depth was not correct. In this way, we can agree with our amendment to this bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e21910	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! We have already passed a number of very, very important laws on road traffic and safety, and today we are adding another important law with the Road Tunnel Safety Act. With this law – a milestone for road safety! – Directive 2004/54/EC on the minimum safety requirements for tunnels in the trans-European road network, the so-called TERN, will be implemented once in Austria. However, in two respects we go beyond the requirements of the directive, first of all with regard to safety measures and safety standards. We are doing more in Austria than the directive requires. After the serious accident in the Tauerntunnel, the transport resort has set up a tunnel safety commission. All the tunnels have been checked, it has been evaluated, here has Council of Ministers Dipl.-Ing. Rudolf Hörhan, an excellent specialist in tunnel safety, deserves to be made, and that is now being implemented. Secondly, with regard to the scope, we go beyond the requirements of the directive. The directive goes in the direction that only all tunnels with more than 500 metres of length need to be included in the TERN network – the regulations for all tunnels apply to motorways and highways over 500 meters. We have time until 2019 to make the legal state, and we have to spend 1.1 billion € on Asfinag funds. So you know how much that is: by €300 million less than the Bawag put in the sand. For example, a lower share of this item concerns the reduction of emergency telephone distances, the reduction of escape distance distances or the complete video surveillance. However, the main costs relate to the construction of the second tunnel tubes, because both according to the directive – we have just passed the provision into the law – and now, according to law, two tunnel tubes have to be built in the case of a traffic volume of more than 10,000 motor vehicles per lane. In particular on the Pyhrn motorway A 9 the Klaus tunnel chain has to be built there, and also the Gleinalm tunnel – that will not be quite cheap – the Rheintal motorway with the Pfänder Tunnel or the Dalaaser Tunnel, Perjen Tunnel, Flirscher Tunnel, to name only the most important. We have also taken particular account of data protection through an amendment in the Committee. This means that video surveillance must be announced, and there are also precise legal requirements for the operation of this surveillance centre. The training of tunnel safety officers has also been clarified. They must be adequately trained according to RVS 14.226. I would like to thank the Transport Agency for the presentation of this law, which really is good for Europe, and I am looking forward to a unanimous adoption.

On October 17, 1973, last revised on October 17, 1973. of 28 December 1991, together with the two decisions of the Administrative Board of 28 December 1991 June 2001 (1380 d.a.)
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e25001	We're now going to the 23rd grade. Agenda item. I should like to ask the ladies and gentlemen to take a seat, and we have a constitutional vote, that is, a quota of 92 Members in the House. There is only one speaker on this point. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Rester-Hinterseer has spoken. She wants to talk to us for 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00142_d7e25032	Mr President. Estimated Secretary of State! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! This draft government is about the file to revise the Convention on the Grant of European Patents. We are in principle in favour of a revision of the EPC and also of adapting to international developments such as the TRIPS Agreement. However, at the then conference in Munich in front of the European Patent Office, we also expressed our massive concerns, which have still not been eliminated over the years. We have discussed in the committee whether there are patents on human organisms and living beings. This has been denied. It is, however, true that patents were granted to human beings and human-animal actors. As early as 1999, the EPO granted a patent for a mixture. That was a Australian company, the company AMRAD, which has extracted gene parts from embryonic stem cells of humans, mice, birds, sheep, pigs, cattle, goats or fish and has patented them for the production of foams. We believe that this form of patent allocation must be deeply rejected. This is also surprising because the EPO declared publicly in October 2000 that such patents would violate the good practices, but that it could do nothing more about them now. The same thing then took place again in February of next year, that is, 2000. A patent was granted on human embryos. First, the EPO denied this – but it was actually found – but then said that this patenting was an error. However, this error cannot be reversed. What are our demands? – Our demands remain that genes, plants, animals, people and parts of the human body should not be patented at all. The Member States of the European Patent Convention, including Austria, must, as political control, ensure that the European Patent Office no longer grant any further patents on life. We also call for an independent, democratic and publicly comprehensible control body to be established in order to prevent future events as they have occurred in the European Patent Office in recent years. We will continue to monitor this together with the European Greens in the European Parliament.
